Having reached the age of one hundred and twenty years, he gathered the people on the other side of the Jordan, and blessed the twelve tribes. Just like Jacob, exactly, under the veil of mystery, he too announced who would be, in these same tribes, the Son blessed in secret by the Father, the moment not yet having come when this Son could, in the flesh, appear in the world.

This Moses is born - in the land of Egypt, at the time when the sons of Israel were emigrants there - of the tribe of Levi, of a father Amram, of a mother Jochebed. He has a brother, Aaron, and a sister, Miriam. Exposed on the banks of the river at the age of three months, placed in a little basket and, by the daughter of Pharaoh removed from there, he is raised in the house of the king. This Moses is born - in the land of Egypt, at the time when the sons of Israel were emigrants there - of the tribe of Levi, of a father Amram, of a mother Jochebed. Then, having reached the age of forty years, he decided to go make a tour among the sons of Israel, his brothers. Having seen a Hebrew harshly beaten, he came to his rescue, by striking down the Egyptian. And Pharaoh, having learned of it, sought to kill him: and he, having taken flight, established himself as an emigrant in the land of Midian, and tended the flocks of Jethro his father-in-law.

Then, having reached the age of eighty years, he led the people out of Egypt on the order of God, after having brought upon it ten plagues. Now those who, thanks to faith, were called, behold, in their own person, they were the types, objects of the blessing they had received by mouth (312), to such an extent that, having become sons of the New Testament, of the Holy Land, which (God) had promised in advance, they will be found to be heirs; and that those who, taking the opposite path, were not justified by the works of the Law, and did not, by faith, after having done penance, glorify Christ, will be, since they condemned themselves, justly tormented. "Here is the blessing that Moses, man of God, pronounced while blessing the sons of Israel. And, before he died, he said: "The Lord [came from Sinai] (318), and he appeared to us from Seir, and in haste he came from the mountain of Paran, with the myriads of Kadesh. At his right hand are Angels with him. And he spared (the defeat of) his people. And all those who are sanctified are under your hands, and these are under you. And he received from his [words] (319) the Law that Moses prescribed to us as an inheritance for the assemblies of Jacob. And there will be, with the Beloved, a prince, when the princes of the peoples gather with the tribes of Israel.

By saying this, the Prophet brings back before our eyes the help that effectively came to the people from God in the wars of foreign nations, led by Sihon, king of Heshbon, and by Og, king of Bashan, wars in which he (Yahweh) appeared to them with Angels to save them from their enemies, thus sparing his people (from defeat). "Here is the blessing that Moses, man of God, pronounced while blessing the sons of Israel. And, before he died, he said: "The Lord [came from Sinai] (318), and he appeared to us from Seir, and in haste he came from the mountain of Paran, with the myriads of Kadesh. At his right hand are Angels with him. And he spared (the defeat of) his people. And all those who are sanctified are under your hands, and these are under you. And he received from [his] (319) words the Law that Moses prescribed to us as an inheritance for the assemblies of Jacob. And there will be, with the Beloved, a prince, when the princes of the peoples gather with the tribes of Israel.

It is possible to understand this also of the future coming of the Lord. For he who, on Mount Sinai, appeared to Moses, he, with Angels, will come and will save the Saints from their persecuting and oppressing enemies, thus sparing (from defeat) those who will have hoped in Him. For he says:

brought upon the land of the Egyptians, I will not bring upon you, because I am the Lord your God. Now, who was the Lord who was speaking there with Moses, if not the Son? Or whose was the Law that he was prescribing, if not the Father's? Which Lord, because he wished to show the (power) (325) that is personally his to judge, said: "All the plagues that I brought upon Egypt, I will not bring upon you." And he did not say: "that He (the Father) brought," because "the Father judges no one, but has given all judgment to the Son," who judged the Egyptians by the Red Sea, having justly submerged them there.

cannot have dominion," said: "Let him live and not die." For David, too, it was not of himself that he could say: "I shall live and not die," he who indeed "is dead; and his tomb is there in our midst to this day." But he "also, in advance, saw the resurrection of the Lord (331)," and for those who were to believe he was prophesying. Furthermore, the Apostle says: "He who is dead, has died to sin once for all, and those who live, live for God (332)." And as for what he says: "To sin he has died once for all," this means that, all men being sinners (333), He whom death precisely did not overcome, "for us all, once for all, he has died, so that those who are living may no longer live for themselves, but for Him who for them has died and risen." And "if we die under the baptism of Christ, we believe that, of his resurrection also, we become participants; knowing well this,

"To sin he has died once for all," this means that, all men being sinners (333), He whom death precisely did not overcome, "for us all, once for all, he has died, so that those who are living may no longer live for themselves, but for Him who for them has died and risen." And "if we die under the baptism of Christ, we believe that, of his resurrection also, we become participants; knowing well this,

that Christ, risen from the dead, dies no more, and that death over him has no more dominion." This was what Moses also said: "Let him live and not die! And let him be, in number, a multitude."

every way, to persuade the generations of men. And this not without reason. On the contrary, knowing well that the generation of men has difficult faith, seeing that they also have free will and that easily every man is inclined to evil rather than to good, for that reason the Spirit strives, through all Scriptures, to make revelations concerning Christ, in order to persuade those who hear (read) them to believe what they say, by which they will be able to live. And when He says: "Hear, Lord, the voice of Judah," He shows (us) Him who, from Judah, according to the flesh, was to be born, the Christ who supplicates the Father by praying continually for the Saints and commends them to the Father. And in the Gospel likewise it is written: "The Lord, having raised his eyes toward Heaven, said: 'Father, I give you thanks. I know that you always hear me, and now it is for this multitude that is around me that I have said (it), so that it might know that it is you who sent me.'" And again: "Holy Father, keep them from the world. It is for them that I pray, and not only for them, but for those who in the future will believe in your name, through me." In the same

way Moses says: "Hear, Lord, the voice of Judah, and may they come to his people!" What are those that will come to his people, if not the blessings and the prayers that the Son was making to the Father for the Saints? And as for what he said: "His hands will judge for him," these are the hands that were nailed to the wood; these are they that judge the world by showing to all then the marks of the nails in his hands, as John also says: "The eyes of all those who pierced him will see him." And again (Moses) says: "Help him to escape from his enemies," because the Father helped him and saved him from his enemies and from the deceivers,

as Jacob also says: "Your hands (be) on the back of your enemies." And David says: "Many dogs have surrounded me, and assemblies of the wicked have besieged me," and: "You, Lord, do not distance your help," "because in you I was cast from the womb, from my mother's belly you are my refuge." It is indeed He whom the Father, once he had exalted him, made to sit on the throne of his glory, to whom he speaks when he says: "Sit at my right hand until I place your enemies as a footstool for your feet." It was therefore indeed He of whom Moses said in advance: "Hear, Lord, the voice of Judah, and may they come to his people, and may his hands judge for him, and help him to escape from his enemies." as Jacob also says: "Your hands (be) on the back of your enemies." And David says: "Many dogs have surrounded me, and assemblies of the wicked have besieged me," and: "You, Lord, do not distance your help," "because in you I was cast from the womb, from my mother's belly you are my refuge." It is indeed He whom the Father, once he had exalted him, made to sit on the throne of his glory, to whom he speaks when he says: "Sit at my right hand until I place your enemies as a footstool for your feet." It was therefore indeed He of whom Moses said in advance: "Hear, Lord, the voice of Judah, and may they come to his people, and may his hands judge for him, and help him to escape from his enemies."

He who said to his father and to his mother: 'I have not seen you,' and, to his brothers: 'I have not known (you),' has indeed also known (337) his sons. He has kept your words; and your covenant, he has not transgressed. They will make known your justices (335) to Jacob, and your Law, to Israel. They will put incense in your anger, perpetually, on your altar. Bless, Lord, his strength, and the works of his hands, receive them. Break the strength of those who have risen against him, and may those who hate him not rise again." They blasphemed him near (336) the water of contention. He who said to his father and to his mother: 'I have not seen you,' and, to his brothers: 'I have not known (you),' has indeed also known (337) his sons. He has kept your words; and your covenant, he has not transgressed. They will make known your justices (335) to Jacob, and your Law, to Israel. They will put incense in your anger, perpetually, on your altar. Bless, Lord, his strength, and the works of his hands, receive them. Break the strength of those who have risen against him, and may those who hate him not rise again."

And by saying: "And his truth to the holy man," it is as a man again that he made him appear, (a man) who was to appear in the last times, as Peter also cried out with all frankness to the princes of the people, when he said: "Jesus of Nazareth, the man to whom, for you, from God testimony was rendered, by miracle, and by signs and wonders that God performed through him in your midst, it is he, once he had been delivered by virtue of the well-determined plan and foreknowledge of God, (he) whom after having, by impious hands, nailed to (the cross), you killed, (it is he) whom God has raised up, freeing him from the cruel pains of the underworld."

And Moses says: "Give to Levi his blessings, and his justices to the holy man." And so that no one might go imagining Christ as being only a simple man, Moses took the precaution of saying: "Whom they put to the test in the trial. They blasphemed him near (336) the waters of contention." Now where did they put him to the test and blaspheme him if not in the desert, when the people murmured against Moses and Aaron and said: "Give us water so that we may drink! Because you have brought us out of Egypt to kill us and our sons by thirst." And Moses said to them: "Why do you blaspheme against us and put the Lord to the test?" "It is not against us that your murmur goes, but against God." The Lord said to Moses: "I have heard the murmur of the sons of Israel that they have murmured against you." "And now take with you some of the Elders at daybreak, and I will stand on the rock, at Horeb, until you have arrived. And you will strike the rock with the rod with which you struck the river, and there will gush forth waters from the rock and will drink this

not only man, and if, to Moses, in the desert, in speaking (to him), he appeared, he was not man only. This one, the Apostle, because, with evidence, he knew that he is God also, said: "They drank from the Spiritual Rock, and the Rock was Christ himself." We set this forth in detail because of the perverse spirits who, either make the divinity of Christ vanish, or subject the Father of all to the tortures of the Passion, for not having looked at the Scriptures in all simplicity of spirit, (all people) who set snares where their soul dies strangled. Now who was in reality the one who said to Moses: "I will stand on the rock, at Horeb, until you have arrived," if not the one of whom Moses says: "Give to Levi his revelations, and his justice to the holy man whom they put to the test in the trial. Savior did not recognize, because they were not truly brothers. Some were born from the seed of Joseph, but He, from a virgin and the Holy Spirit. They looked at themselves as being his brothers and He did not recognize them. "And his sons, he says, he knew well (342)." What sons, if not the nation of the sons of Israel, whom He called, by the Law, to be, by adoption, his sons? Holy people, nation chosen in preference to all nations, of which he proved that it was specifically his. But for their refusal to acquiesce to his advances, they were no longer recognized by Him. As through Isaiah also, he says: "I have begotten sons, and (have) exalted (them), and they have despised me." "And his sons, he says, he knew well (342)." What sons, if not the nation of the sons of Israel, whom He called, by the Law, to be, by adoption, his sons? Holy people, nation chosen in preference to all nations, of which he proved that it was specifically his. But for their refusal to acquiesce to his advances, they were no longer recognized by Him. As through Isaiah also, he says: "I have begotten sons, and (have) exalted (them), and they have despised me."

What does Moses say? "He kept thy words, and, thy covenant, [he did] not transgress (342)." Who kept? And whose (342) words did he keep? If not, alone, the Son, the words of the Father? (The Son) who came to fulfill the law of the Father and wanted to show in himself the New Covenant? Which He precisely said: "I have not come to do my will, but the will of Him who sent me, of my Father." And again: "Of myself I do nothing, but in the measure that I see my Father doing, in the same measure I do." He therefore indeed kept (342) his words, and his covenant, he did not transgress (342). In the same way also, through Zechariah, the Father says to him: "In my commandments

embracing the other, he pierced them with his sword, and, once he had thus removed the iniquities, by hand, he showed (them) to the people (349). If therefore it is so, when someone kills the sinner, he is not condemned with the murderers; but he who, in contempt of justice, kills the just man, he is condemned as a murderer. For, indeed, the princes of this world kill sinners every day, by condemning (them) by virtue of their law, and, for murder, they are not justiciable. If therefore someone, in contempt of justice, kills another, that one is a murderer, and, as a murderer, he will be condemned. And Simeon, (by Moses), for the aforementioned reasons, once left behind (350), it is necessary, regarding him, to give a double interpretation. For we find that the blessed Joseph had Simeon put in bonds in the presence of his brothers, and put him in prison for three days, to whom (Simeon), furthermore, he said: "You will not go out from here until your youngest brother has arrived here." Now what could well be the reasons he had for putting Simeon in bonds and no one else? It is because, when Joseph was sent to them, it is he (Simeon) who plotted with his brothers, and said: "Come, let us kill him, and we shall see what becomes of his dreams." Judah orders to sell him

has arrived here." The moment when, both Simeon was unbound from (his) bonds and the brothers, with him (352), were blessed by Joseph, is the moment when their youngest brother had arrived in Egypt, who was precisely Christ, by whom is made remission of transgressions to those who believe in Him, and by whom (353) those who, "in bonds and shadows of death remained caught," were delivered from torments, and (by whom) blessing, to all, is sent from the heavens. For Simeon, he too, once he had recognized Christ in the Temple, said while prophesying: "Now you let your servant go, Lord, according to your word, in peace." According to what word, if not the one that, through Joseph, he said: "You will not go out from here until your youngest brother has arrived here." If therefore it is so, it is not only for the twelve tribes that the life-giving Savior

impossible for men to rise from the dead, and behold that Christ is risen from the dead, so that, in him (354), our resurrection might be made visible. It was impossible for men to ascend to the Heavens, and behold that Christ reigns in the Heavens, so that, in him (354), our future "citizenship in the Heavens" might be made manifest. It was impossible for man, who is born in corruption, to live eternally, and behold that this has been done in Christ (354). And so that we might believe in the future life of the holy bodies, (life) incorruptible and eternal, now, "what could not be done by men, that has been able to be done by God." And who is He who operates this, if not the Son? Or whose will is this, if not the Father's, by whose will man lives? And he who, previously, in disobedience, was hated by God, now, in obedience, becomes, for God, worthy of love. It is also because everything had to be prefigured by the Law and the Prophets, so that, when the Truth (351) had come, all this might be recognized, and that what was lacking from there (the Law and the Prophets), She might complete it by the Gospel. impossible for men to rise from the dead, and behold that Christ is risen from the dead, so that, in him (354), our resurrection might be made visible. It was impossible for men to ascend to the Heavens, and behold that Christ reigns in the Heavens, so that, in him (354), our future "citizenship in the Heavens" might be made manifest. It was impossible for man, who is born in corruption, to live eternally, and behold that this has been done in Christ (354). And so that we might believe in the future life of the holy bodies, (life) incorruptible and eternal, now, "what could not be done by men, that has been able to be done by God." And who is He who operates this, if not the Son? Or whose will is this, if not the Father's, by whose will man lives? And he who, previously, in disobedience, was hated by God, now, in obedience, becomes, for God, worthy of love.

Now who was the beloved of the Lord, if not Paul the Apostle who "was born from the womb of Benjamin" and by the Lord, as "a chosen instrument," was chosen and, by the world, herald of truth, was sent?

"He shall dwell," he says, "in hope." Where in reality did he dwell? It is quite evident that it is in the Church, once he had abandoned the Synagogue; as also the Apostle himself says: "For the advantage that I had from the Law, I count it, because of Christ, as loss. Moreover, still, I count everything as loss, in order to gain Christ and to be found in Him, having, not my own righteousness, which comes from the Law, but that which comes from faith in Jesus Christ." Moses said well: "He shall dwell in hope, and God shall be a protective shadow for him all the days." While he was going to Damascus, the Lord appeared to him and was for him a protective shadow, and by the power of the Lord he performed miracles, and, by his name, in all the cities, he accomplished miracles.

"And for Joseph he said: [Of the blessing] of the Lord (receives) his land, [at the hour of Heaven] and by the dew, and from the sources of the abysses (springing) from beneath the earth, according to [the hour, the grains] of the solstices of the sun, and during the months which go on adding themselves one to the other, and from the summit of the mountains from the beginning and from the summit of the eternal hills, and [at the hour] of fulfillment. And may the complacencies of Him who appeared from [within] the bush come upon the head of Joseph, and upon the summit (of the forehead) of him who is glorified in the midst of brothers. First-born of a bull: beauty belongs to him. Horns of the unicorn (are) his horns! He will gore the nations with them (356) unto the ends of the earth. These are the myriads of Ephraim, and these are the thousands of Manasseh (357)."

It is possible to understand it of this very One, the Savior (362), for it was said: "Of the blessing of the Lord (receives) his land," because, born of the Virgin, from the Holy Spirit he had as an inheritance all the blessing of the Lord, the "Holy Land" (his Humanity), which became visible in the last times, by which he signifies that what was for the Fathers "Land of promise" is nothing other than This (this Holy Land which is the Incarnate Word). For, furthermore, God had said to them: "I will give you the land which makes honey and milk gush forth, which I promised to give to your fathers"—which signifies the exit of the Word out of the Father—(363) of which (fathers) the Savior, subsequently, once born, appeared as "Land" or "Way" toward the Father, having in himself the milk: the Old Testament and the Legislation; the honey: the sweetness of the Word, by which he sweetens the bitterness of our hearts. And: "According to the hour, the grains of the solstices of the sun (368) and during the months which go on adding themselves one to the other," because He, "Sun," once he had risen from the bosom of the earth, showed the twelve Apostles (as) twelve "hours"; for it is by them that the "Day" will manifest itself, as the Prophet says: "This is the Day which the Lord has made." And as for what he said: "During the months which go on adding themselves," (it is that), once gathered together, the twelve Apostles as twelve "Months," announced the "Perfect Year": Christ. The prophet, he too, says: "Proclaim a year of grace of the Lord, and a day of retribution." And, because "Day," and "Sun," and "Year" was Christ, one must call the Apostles "Hours" and "Months."

Moses says: "… from the summit of the mountains from the beginning, and from the summit of the eternal hills." By "summit of the mountains" he said the Patriarchs, because

მთანი თასი გიგა შიისს აღი აყრის ხი ს გათვა შიისს; ტელიე მღისის- უეღებრა–ეგენბგბს.-–.–- იგინი არიან წინაისწარ. პორცუს სამარადისო წინაისწარმეტყუელთა მათ სახელ- ს–დებს. რამეთუ სიტყუაი იგი დასაბამითგან მამათ–მთავართა გან გამოიხატა. და წინაისწარმეტყუელთა მიერ მიმოედვა და ჟამსა მას აღსრულებისასა. ვითარცა აღმასრულებელი შჯულისაი მის და წინაისწარმეტყუელთა იგი არს. რომელი
they are prior; by "eternal hills" he named the Prophets, because the Word, from the beginning, was figured by the Patriarchs, then, by the Prophets, announced. And, "at the hour of fulfillment," as fulfiller of the Law and the Prophets, came He who, through the Prophet, says: "I who precisely spoke to you, I am near as the hour on the mountains."

He who has, all together, redeemed his people from Egypt, who was angered against Pharaoh and covered the Egyptians with confusion and delivered the Saints from their servitude, it is He who in advance was figured by Joseph and "glorified among brothers" and "loved by the Father." It is of him that Moses says: "First-born of a bull: beauty belongs to him!" Because as a "sacred bull-calf," as a victim, offered to God, invested with what, of the two Testaments, was to be fulfilled (370), he ruminated continually the power of the Spirit.

Because he will make you suck the wealth of the sea (374 and 381) and the gains of the merchandise of those who dwell on the shores of the sea (375).'"

And Moses said: "Rejoice, Zebulun, in your going out." "Going out," he said, not "(the going out)" from Egypt, for Zebulun was not the only one to go out then, but all the tribes. This, with a prophetic voice (378), announcing there the good news, the prophet says to all those who have placed their hope in Christ: "Rejoice, Zebulun, in your going out," because those who go out from this world in a state of holiness become joyful because of the hope of the resurrection of the dead. And those who in these gulfs (379) of the Father have found rest, behold, they are sons of resurrection, who are ready to inherit the incorruptible eternity in the Paradise of delights. And Solomon says: "Prepare your works for your going out, and go there, and come after me, and again you will build your house."

He has united (380) Zebulun and Issachar to show justified the two calls to the going out toward Christ: and Zebulun is called "Gracious gift" and Issachar, "Wages," and those who have worked well in the vineyard will receive from God, as a "gracious gift" and "wages," eternal life; as, moreover, for the laborers, he said: "Give them the wages, beginning from the last up to the first."

Dan. "And for Dan he said: "Dan (is) a lion's whelp, and he will leap from Bashan." Because the Scriptures have called Christ "lion" and "lion's whelp" by reason of his royal dignity, and of what he has of power and glory, of the Antichrist also, Moses said the same thing, because the false (Christ) wants, in everything, to make himself like the Son of God. The people believed that he was the Christ; but, for the saint, because he knows in advance, through the Scriptures, his wickedness, he is the Antichrist. "And for Dan he said: "Dan (is) a lion's whelp, and he will leap from Bashan." The people believed that he was the Christ; but, for the saint, because he knows in advance, through the Scriptures, his wickedness, he is the Antichrist.

And Asher is called "Wealth" (398), as it was also previously said: "Wealth of the sea you shall suck (399)," "you," that is to say the nations who draw the commandments of the Lord. That is why he says: "He shall be He whom one must well receive, for his brothers." "For brothers," he said, not according to the flesh, but according to the spirit, (brothers) who believe in Him, to whom the Savior said: "My brothers and co-heirs." For these, He was indeed He whom one must well receive, the Word of God, appeared as man, in the flesh, on the earth. But He is also, by them, confessed God, since Thomas also says: "My Lord and my God." This one, his brothers according to the flesh, not having recognized Him, despised Him, and it is after having

made little account of Him, as of a miserable and poor man, that they despised Him (400). This is also what the Spirit of the Prophet prophesies and he says: "I have begotten sons and raised (them) up, And they have despised me. The ox has recognized its owner and the donkey the manger of its master, And Israel has not recognized me, And my people have not understood me." And John supported these words with his testimony and he says: "And his brothers did not believe in Him." Now for which brothers was He "He whom one must well receive" if not for those who were called from afar, from among the nations (401), (those) to whom He said precisely: "Come to me, all you who are weary and carry a heavy burden, and I will give you rest. teaching, for I am meek and humble of heart, and you will find rest for your souls. For my yoke is easy and my burden, light." And Asher is called "Wealth" (398), as it was also previously said: "Wealth of the sea you shall suck (399)," "you," that is to say the nations who draw the commandments of the Lord. That is why he says: "He shall be He whom one must well receive, for his brothers." "For brothers," he said, not according to the flesh, but according to the spirit, (brothers) who believe in Him, to whom the Savior said: "My brothers and co-heirs." For these, He was indeed He whom one must well receive, the Word of God, appeared as man, in the flesh, on the earth. But He is also, by them, confessed God, since Thomas also says: "My Lord and my God." This one, his brothers according to the flesh, not having recognized Him, despised Him, and it is after having

And so that we might understand of whom he was speaking there, Moses added, saying: "He shall bathe his foot in oil," which, metaphorically and in a parable (402), he said for those who are capable of hearing these words in the strong sense (403). Then all the Saints "will reign with Christ." The Israelites are called (420) "God-Seers" perpetually, for having established themselves (antecedently, during their life), through hope, in the "Holy Land" (i.e., in Christ). For "what one sees, why does one still hope for it? (421)". They (the Saints) are henceforth having nothing more to fear from death (422), and finding no more enjoyment in corruptible foods, and being neither preoccupied with earthly cares, nor weighed down by the burning wind or winter frosts; and they no longer have to cultivate the earth, nor to eat, at the price of efforts and by the sweat of their brow, the earthly bread. For, sin, one speaks of it no more! Finished, the concupiscences! Vanquished is Death! Trampled underfoot is the Seducer! Suppressed (423) is the Tyrant! Killed is the treacherous Serpent! Liberated are the creatures! Wide open

Then all the Saints "will reign with Christ." The Israelites are called (420) "God-Seers" perpetually, for having established themselves (antecedently, during their life), through hope, in the "Holy Land" (i.e., in Christ). For "what one sees, why does one still hope for it? (421)". They (the Saints) are henceforth having nothing more to fear from death (422), and finding no more enjoyment in corruptible foods, and being neither preoccupied with earthly cares,

From then on (428) the Saints, for having tasted of the incorruptible Bread of Life and having drunk of the immortal drink (thereby) constituted (429), by the Word, as spiritual Angels in concert with all creation, will breathe incessant blessing, by glorifying, with a praise complete in all respects (430), the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit, now and always and unto the ages of ages. Amen.
